> "Some say that the Jews were fugitives from the island of Crete, who settled on the nearest coast of Africa about the time when Saturn was driven from his throne by the power of Jupiter. Evidence of this is sought in the name. There is a famous mountain in Crete called Ida; the neighbouring tribe, the Idæi, came to be called Judæi by a barbarous lengthening of the national name. Others assert that in the reign of Isis the overflowing population of Egypt, led by Hierosolymus and Judas, discharged itself into the neighbouring countries. Many, again, say that they were a race of Ethiopian origin, who in the time of king Cepheus were driven by fear and hatred of their neighbours to seek a new dwelling-place. Others describe them as an Assyrian horde who, not having sufficient territory, took possession of part of Egypt, and founded cities of their own in what is called the Hebrew country, lying on the borders of Syria. Others, again, assign a very distinguished origin to the Jews, alleging that they were the Solymi, a nation celebrated in the poems of Homer, who called the city which they founded Hierosolyma after their own name. (Latin)  
> 3  
>   
> Most writers, however, agree in stating that once a disease, which horribly disfigured the body, broke out over Egypt; that king Bocchoris, seeking a remedy, consulted the oracle of Hammon, and was bidden to cleanse his realm, and to convey into some foreign land this race detested by the gods. The people, who had been collected after diligent search, finding themselves left in a desert, sat for the most part in a stupor of grief, till one of the exiles, Moyses by name, warned them not to look for any relief from God or man, forsaken as they were of both, but to trust to themselves, taking for their heaven-sent leader that man who should first help them to be quit of their present misery. They agreed, and in utter ignorance began to advance at random. Nothing, however, distressed them so much as the scarcity of water, and they had sunk ready to perish in all directions over the plain, when a herd of wild asses was seen to retire from their pasture to a rock shaded by trees. Moyses followed them, and, guided by the appearance of a grassy spot, discovered an abundant spring of water. This furnished relief. After a continuous journey for six days, on the seventh they possessed themselves of a country, from which they expelled the inhabitants, and in which they founded a city and a temple. (Latin)  
> 4  
>   
> Moyses, wishing to secure for the future his authority over the nation, gave them a novel form of worship, opposed to all that is practised by other men. Things sacred with us, with them have no sanctity, while they allow what with us is forbidden. In their holy place they have consecrated an image of the animal by whose guidance they found deliverance from their long and thirsty wanderings. They slay the ram, seemingly in derision of Hammon, and they sacrifice the ox, because the Egyptians worship it as Apis. They abstain from swine's flesh, in consideration of what they suffered when they were infected by the leprosy to which this animal is liable. By their frequent fasts they still bear witness to the long hunger of former days, and the Jewish bread, made without leaven, is retained as a memorial of their hurried seizure of corn. We are told that the rest of the seventh day was adopted, because this day brought with it a termination of their toils; after a while the charm of indolence beguiled them into giving up the seventh year also to inaction. But others say that it is an observance in honour of Saturn, either from the primitive elements of their faith having been transmitted from the Idæi, who are said to have shared the flight of that God, and to have founded the race, or from the circumstance that of the seven stars which rule the destinies of men Saturn moves in the highest orbit and with the mightiest power, and that many of the heavenly bodies complete their revolutions and courses in multiples of seven. (Latin)  
> 5  
>   
> This worship, however introduced, is upheld by its antiquity; all their other customs, which are at once perverse and disgusting, owe their strength to their very badness. The most degraded out of other races, scorning their national beliefs, brought to them their contributions and presents. This augmented the wealth of the Jews, as also did the fact, that among themselves they are inflexibly honest and ever ready to shew compassion, though they regard the rest of mankind with all the hatred of enemies. They sit apart at meals, they sleep apart, and though, as a nation, they are singularly prone to lust, they abstain from intercourse with foreign women; among themselves nothing is unlawful. Circumcision was adopted by them as a mark of difference from other men. Those who come over to their religion adopt the practice, and have this lesson first instilled into them, to despise all gods, to disown their country, and set at nought parents, children, and brethren. Still they provide for the increase of their numbers. It is a crime among them to kill any newly-born infant. They hold that the souls of all who perish in battle or by the hands of the executioner are immortal. Hence a passion for propagating their race and a contempt for death. They are wont to bury rather than to burn their dead, following in this the Egyptian custom; they bestow the same care on the dead, and they hold the same belief about the lower world. Quite different is their faith about things divine. The Egyptians worship many animals and images of monstrous form; the Jews have purely mental conceptions of Deity, as one in essence. They call those profane who make representations of God in human shape out of perishable materials. They believe that Being to be supreme and eternal, neither capable of representation, nor of decay. They therefore do not allow any images to stand in their cities, much less in their temples. This flattery is not paid to their kings, nor this honour to our Emperors. From the fact, however, that their priests used to chant to the music of flutes and cymbals, and to wear garlands of ivy, and that a golden vine was found in the temple, some have thought that they worshipped Father Liber, the conqueror of the East, though their institutions do not by any means harmonize with the theory; for Liber established a festive and cheerful worship, while the Jewish religion is tasteless and mean."

> The core courses of study in classic philosophy focus on the major branches of philosophy as developed by ancient Greek and Roman thinkers, primarily covering:  
> ​Metaphysics: The nature of existence, being, and the world (e.g., substance, cause).  
> ​Epistemology: The nature and scope of knowledge, truth, and belief.  
> ​Ethics: The study of morality, how humans should live, and the good life.  
> ​Logic: The study of correct reasoning and argument structure.  
> ​History of Philosophy: Focused on the works of Pre-Socratics, Plato, Aristotle, and Hellenistic schools like Stoicism and Epicureanism.  
> ​Many programs also include Aesthetics (the nature of beauty and art) and Political Philosophy (justice and governance).  
> In times past, getting any degree could get you a job. Philosophy majors could be editors but mostly college professors. But as you see you can do most of this studying on your own. Israel will never become the hegemon of West Asia, let alone replace the United States on the global stage. The idea of a “Pax Judaica” is not just unrealistic, it is strategically incoherent. Israel is deeply dependent on the United States. Its military power rests heavily on American support: financial assistance, advanced weaponry, technological transfers, and, perhaps most critical of all, intelligence.

From the F-35 to the American ELINT satellite network, from aerial refueling capabilities to large-scale production of JDAM munitions, all of it is American-made and provided to Israel as part of an unprecedented military partnership.

The recent twelve-day conflict with Iran highlighted this dependence. The operation was a textbook example of NATO-level coordination: U.S. THAAD and Patriot systems, American radar sites and satellites tracking Iranian launches in real time, and even French and British aircraft contributing to the interception of Iranian drones. Israel did not stand alone; it was shielded by a broad coalition of Western intelligence and defense assets.

Yet despite all this, Israel failed to break Iran’s strategic posture and suffered significant strikes on its own territory. If that is the outcome with the full backing of the United States and its allies, a new war would likely only push Israel into an even worse position.

Looking ahead, Iran is set to acquire more modern fighter aircraft and reinforce its air-defense network. With the rise of the BRICS bloc and shifting global power dynamics, Iran, a natural regional power with demographic, geographic, and industrial depth, will continue to assert itself and grow stronger.

These factors point to a simple conclusion: Israel will always face Iran as a systemic rival. The notion of Israel dominating West Asia, or replacing the United States as a global power, has no grounding in strategic reality.

### **@motionislife4879** | 👍 1 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3t0MeVBD24I&lc=UgzzW_B8318PCx-G_-J4AaABAg)

Upanishads define Brahman as the eternal, infinite, unchanging absolute reality—formless (nirguna), beyond senses, the essence (sat-chit-ananda: existence, consciousness, bliss) underlying all creation, self, and phenomena, often via negations like neti neti (not this, not that). It is the singular (ekam eva advitiyam: one without a second) source that creates, sustains, and dissolves the universe, identical with Atman (individual self), realized through direct knowledge (jnana) for liberation (moksha).​

Key descriptions in Upanishads
Brihadaranyaka: Truth, infinite, breath of life, inner controller pervading all.​

Chandogya: "Tat Tvam Asi" (You are That)—subtle essence of everything.​

Mandukya: Pure consciousness (Turiya), beyond waking/dream/sleep states, symbolized by Om.​

Common traits: Omnipresent, omniscient, pure, fearless, indivisible, not an object of perception but the perceiver.​

Differences from Monad
Brahman differs from the Monad (Pythagorean/Neoplatonic "One")—a static, ineffable first principle emanating the cosmos hierarchically through Nous (Intellect) and Soul—primarily in immanence and self-realization: Brahman is dynamically identical with all selves (Atman=Brahman), experientially known via meditation, not just rationally contemplated as a transcendent unity beyond multiplicity. While both denote ultimate oneness, Monad lacks Brahman's explicit consciousness-bliss (chit-ananda) and soteriological path of inner awakening; Brahman integrates personal (saguna) forms (e.g., as Ishvara) alongside impersonal essence.

### **@onnixchaney8797** | 👍 3 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3t0MeVBD24I&lc=Ugx1O7iprgISci3qUP54AaABAg)

What you’re saying here sounds bold but it falls apart the second you touch real history.
	1.	“Every successful power used total war, killed civilians, destroyed cities, killed gods, etc.”
 
That’s just not true. 
The early Muslim conquests alone blow this up. The Rashidun Caliphate took down the entire Sasanian Empire and huge chunks of Byzantium in a few decades with explicit rules against killing non-combatants, destroying crops, or targeting civilians. 
Same with a lot of Chinese dynasties, the Ottomans, and even the British Empire – most of their power came from administration, trade, and alliances, not exterminating populations and burning everything to the ground.


	2.	“You have to be meritocratic or you lose.”
Plenty of rigid, aristocratic systems won wars and built empires: 
 Achaemenid Persians, many European monarchies, Mughals, etc. 

Yes, promoting talent helps, but it’s not some iron law. States have often sabotaged talented generals for political reasons and still won because they had more resources, better logistics, or better timing. 
 You’re taking something that’s sometimes true and preaching it like a universal rule.


	3.	“Kill their gods to win.”
Historically, most empires did the opposite. 
Persians let people keep their religions. Romans absorbed local gods into their own pantheon. Ottomans protected Christian and Jewish communities. The Mongols literally had Buddhists, Muslims, and Christians all in their orbit. 

Religious tolerance (or at least pragmatism) was usually smart statecraft. 
This “kill their gods” line sounds edgy but it’s not how most successful powers actually operated.
 
	4.	“If they don’t do these things, they’re not really at war, they’re friends.”

That’s just redefining words to fit your narrative. 

Most wars in history have been limited wars:
 border disputes, succession conflicts, punitive expeditions, proxy wars, etc. 

Limited objectives, limited destruction. 
 Saying “if it’s not total annihilation, it’s fake” is circular:
 any counterexample just gets waved away as “not a real war.” That’s not analysis, that’s a trick.

So the pattern here is 
cherry-pick the most brutal examples (Mongols, Aztecs), exaggerate what they did, ignore all the counterexamples, then use that to push a “they’re all secretly friends” conclusion. 

It sounds dramatic, but it’s not how history actually works.

> **@onnixchaney8797** | 👍 1 likes  
> [View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3t0MeVBD24I&lc=Ugx1O7iprgISci3qUP54AaABAg.AQEAoc53vyAAQECXQobuAH)  
>
> A lot of what you’re saying here sounds impressive until you actually know the history of the period you’re talking about. Almost every claim in this section is either oversimplified, backwards, or just flat-out wrong.  
>   
> 1. “Upper nobility vs lower nobility is the universal engine of empire building.”  
> Not really. Mesopotamian city-states weren’t structured like Rome, and trying to force the Roman social template backwards onto Sumer is just bad history. Early Sumerian politics revolved around priestly authority, temple economies, and city cult, not the Roman “upper vs lower nobility” model you’re projecting.  
>   
> 2. “Lugalzaggesi succeeded because he ransacked temples.”  
> Temple-raiding wasn’t an innovation.  
>  It was a symptom of collapsing city-state stability.  
>  The whole late Early Dynastic period is full of cities looting each other’s holy sites. He wasn’t special,  he was the last gasp of that political model.  
>   
> 3. “Sargon was probably a mercenary who stole the throne like David.”  
> This is pure speculation dressed up as “pattern recognition.”  
> Sargon wasn’t a mercenary.  
> He was a palace official from Kish who overthrew the previous ruler. Mixing David into Mesopotamian history is just story-telling, not scholarship.  
>   
> 4. “Outsiders unify because insiders are stuck in equilibrium.”  
> Again, sounds clever, not accurate. Outside shocks unify regions when trade networks collapse, ecologies shift, or city-states burn themselves out.   
> The Akkadian rise fits all of that  not some magical “mercenary outsider always wins” formula. The Amorites did it. The Kassites did it. The Arameans did it.   
>   
> This isn’t destiny, it’s late-stage urban fragility.  
>   
> 5. “Qin won China because they practiced ‘total warfare’ and didn’t respect the rules.”  
> This is another myth.   
>  Qin succeeded because they had:  
> – the strongest agrarian reforms,  
> – the harshest legalist bureaucracy,  
> – the best iron weapon production,  
> – mass conscription,  
> – and the most efficient administrative system.  
>   
> “Total warfare” is a YouTube buzzword, not an actual explanation.  
>   
> 6. “The Akkadian Empire conquered the Levant.”  
> No. The Akkadian sphere influenced parts of the Levant, yes, but portraying it like they fully conquered all of Anatolia and the Levant is just wrong.   
>  The archaeological and inscriptional record is mixed, not the giant blob map you’re imagining.  
>   
> 7. “Whoever controls the Levant always threatens Egypt.”  
> Only sometimes. Egypt wasn’t constantly trembling because somebody held Byblos. Egypt lost and regained Levantine influence dozens of times depending on the dynasty, the Nile floods, and internal stability. You’re compressing 2,000 years of very different geopolitical conditions into a single dramatic sentence.  
>   
> 8. “Jerusalem is the center of the world for geopolitical reasons.”  
> Historically? No. Jerusalem mattered religiously, not geopolitically. The ancient superpowers cared far more about Megiddo, Kadesh, Gaza, and Tyre. Jerusalem became central in a religious sense, not a logistical one. This is another modern trope being projected backwards.

### **@Politicianist**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3t0MeVBD24I&lc=UgxQp7Ju92xMTbeXMst4AaABAg)

I think it actually confuses to look at the Israeli-Palestinian conflict from a religious perspective. Because the origins of Zionism are in the mid-19th century national-liberal movements of Europe. Zionism was Austrian Jewish lawyer Theodor Herzl's answer to the question of "If Czechs, Hungarians, Serbs, Germans and Italians get to have their own Nation-State, what about the Jewish nation?". By this point, the religious aspect serves as a compass, but not as a structural explainer for why Israel behaves as it does (ie pushing for Greater Israel, having both secular and orthodox components to its charter, etc.) and it's because Israel is operating in a 19th century nationalist/expansionist project that would be very familiar to basically every other nation in Europe who got their nation-state post-WW1 and also post-WW2. But since Israel is very late to the game and since their foundation came in the immediate aftermath of WW2, they have been in a struggle with American-Soviet hegemony with the Americans trying to make Israel work for them, and the Israelis wanting to operate free of imperial interference, thus in some ways Israel is in rebellion of the international liberal order in a way that's not too disimilar from say, China.

### **@am7535**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3t0MeVBD24I&lc=Ugy7EaayXZ-byUD3i1d4AaABAg)

Very good lecture Prof but allow me a few remarks:
1. Canaanites first ruled Egypt bef the opposite occurred from 1750-1550BC (Hyksos empire).
2. Israelites were at first a Canaanite solar priesthood of the southern hinterland based in the Galilee (most probably around Hazor & Megiddo) where the future kingdom of Israel-Samaria was founded around 1000BC.
3. Around 1300-1400BC the Apiru semi nomad Hebrew marauders allied with the Shady nomad of Yahweh from the Negev & Sinai and took over the Canaanite hinterland and the Israelite Canaanite priesthood but the Shasus imposed their deity Yahweh while the original Israelites followed El. Samaritans & especially Jews were at the beginning an ethnic group who emerged as an intermediate group from the Arameans or Amorites and the Canaanites. They probably started as an Aramean pastoral group of Amurru (north west Syria) who commuted with Mesopotamia referred by Akkadians as « Martu » (« westerners » as in west of the Euphrates) also called « Amorites » and smts as « Ahlamu » tribes which included Amorites, Arameans, and Chaldeans. Those people invaded and ruled Assur and Babel since King Hammurabi who was an Amorites.
4. «Israel » really springs from « Yishrael » meaning « El rules » in Canaanite and is not related to Yahweh which is also a Canaanite deity but equivalent to Baal which became the enemy of El, his father.
5. Actually, El represented the solar priesthood and Baal the lunar priesthood which clashed over sexual rites or matters of a sexual nature determining the rapport between man and woman and the authority of one with respect to the other. 
6. Yahweh is really the equivalent of Baal and he didn’t clash with El for Samaritans and Jews but originally is not El and he is really hostile to El.
7. Israelites’ successors are really Samaritans while Jews are Yahweh worshipers and were only scripturally linked to Israel but politically they were always on their own except perhaps of under David & Solomon but surely after Solomon Jews were always separate from and enemies to sraelite Samaritans and never did they try to use Israel as the name of their kingdom until the British promoted it in the 19-20th century.

As this conversion progressed, the whole cause behind Israel, its insistence on maintaining a distinct identity from its neighbours, would gradually disintegrate, as Israelis just view their neighbours as one and the same people with themselves. Collective guilt & monoliths: Despite a token correction, you repeatedly treat “the Jews” as a single, trans-historical strategic actor. That is textbook antisemitic framing. No serious historian does this.

Invented intent: Claims like “the Persians invented the Jews” or “Britain recreated Israel using the Bible as a script” are asserted without primary evidence. Pattern-matching is not proof. Similarity ≠ conspiracy.

>
> The Bible presents the Godhead as consisting of two divine persons: the Father and the Son, who together share the nature and authority of God. Scripture explains that God’s invisible power and Godhead are evident from creation (Romans 1:20), and the opening of John’s Gospel clarifies that the Word existed with God and was God from the beginning, participating in the creation of all things (John 1:1–3). This Word is later revealed to be Jesus Christ, the Son of God, showing that both the Father and the Son share divine identity and eternal existence. Even in the earliest chapters of Genesis, God speaks in plural language—“Let us make man in our image” (Genesis 1:26)—suggesting a shared divine work within the Godhead.  
>   
> Throughout Scripture, the Father and the Son are shown as distinct persons who work together in the plan of salvation. The Father sends the Son into the world (1 John 4:14; John 3:16), and Jesus continually testifies that he was sent by the Father to fulfill the Father’s will (John 6:39; John 5:37). The Son is recognized as divine—called the Holy One of God (Luke 4:34), worshiped by angels (Hebrews 1:5–7), and identified as the Word of God who judges and reigns (Revelation 19:10–13). Prophetic passages also speak of a divine Redeemer, the Holy One of Israel, who is both Creator and Savior (Isaiah 47:4; Isaiah 54:5), further connecting the identity of God with the work later fulfilled through Christ.  
>   
> Taken together, these passages describe a unified divine relationship: the Father as the eternal source and sender, and the Son as the eternal Word and Redeemer. They are not the same person, yet they share the same divine nature, authority, and purpose. This relationship is referred to in Scripture as the Godhead, the divine unity through which God creates, reveals Himself, and accomplishes redemption for the world.

### **@johnsalamito6212**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3t0MeVBD24I&lc=Ugygfy8TDd2zrAGc3ZN4AaABAg)

This is Genesis 15:18

“In the same day the LORD made a covenant with Abram, saying, Unto thy seed have I given this land, from the river of Egypt unto the great river, the river Euphrates:”

This is the centrepiece of the Zionist claim expressed in this video. The claim that God promised land to a particular people and God keeps his promise.  Ergo, modern Israel is a biblical fulfilment. So called Christian Zionism.  

This is not Catholic teaching. Despite what the Catholic guest says.  

To understand read what Paul says about this verse. It was equally controversial in his day (there is no Jew or Greek, etc) so he goes out of his way to explicitly explain to the knuckleheads of Galatia in 3:16:

“Now to Abraham and his seed were the promises made. He saith not, And to seeds, as of many; but as of one, And to thy seed, which is Christ.”

The  Bible is thus clear. The promise is not broken but fulfilled in one person - Jesus. He is the seed. 

Note Abram of Genesis was also the father of Arab nations by Ishmael as well and Israel via Isaac.  They were both descendants. But the promise was made to ALL nations via the chosen Abram and this promise is unilaterally fulfilled as prophesied in Genesis 15. After this Abrahamic Covenant a conditional convenant was made through Moses (see Deut 28). The curse of the Mosaic covenant was absorbed by the Cross, which simultaneously fulfilled the eternal Abrahamic covenant. 

Hence Jesus gives the great commission before ascending to heaven to his apostles in Matthew 28:

“Go ye therefore, and teach all nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Ghost:”

Thus the Catholic Church does not believe the promise of land to one people’s is the name of the game, but the baptism of ALL nations, including Jews. 

Israel is a legitimate nation state according natural law as well as UN decree. But it is not a gift of God to Jews.  No, it was the Hebrew that was chosen to deliver the true gift which is Jesus and the Holy Spirit he sent.  

For clarity, I support Israel but not because it is a gift from God to Jews. I challenge the guest to reject the verses above or say that I am misusing them.  Visitors to this channel need to know the truth, not the political obfuscation.
